A Computation of the Action of the Morava Stabilizer Group on the Lubin-Tate Deformation Ring Thanks: This material is based upon work supported by the National Science Foundation under Grant No. DMS-1906227.
André Davis

Abstract
. We compute recursive approximations of the action of the height h ≥ 2 \mathchar 29032\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722 Morava stabilizer group on the associated Lubin-Tate deformation ring. We then specialize to the case h = 3 \mathchar 29032\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28723 and p > 2 \mathchar 29040\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28722 to calculate the action explicitly. These results are new for h > 2 \mathchar 29032\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28722 and agree with computations by Lader at height h = 2 \mathchar 29032\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722 .



1.
Introduction

For a fixed prime
\mathchar 29040
and an integer
\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722
, we let
\mathbb{\mathchar 29000}_{\mathchar 29032}
denote the Honda formal group law with coefficients in the finite field
\mathbb{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 29040^{\mathchar 29032}}
. We are interested in its group of automorphisms, and in particular how this group acts on the Lubin-Tate deformation ring. This action appears frequently in the literature and has been described by Devinatz and Hopkins
[
4
]
as “the most important group action in the whole chromatic approach to stable homotopy theory”.

It is well known that the Lubin-Tate deformation ring
\mathchar 29010_{\mathchar 29032}
is isomorphic to

\mathchar 29010_{\mathchar 29032}\mathrel{\lower 0.5pt\vbox{\halign{$\m@th\displaystyle\hfil#\hfil$\cr\mathchar 536\crcr\mathchar 61\crcr}}}\mathbb{\mathchar 29015}\delimiter 67482370\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 314\mathchar 314\mathchar 314\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84267779

where
\mathbb{\mathchar 29015}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67273472\mathbb{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 29040^{\mathchar 29032}}\delimiter 84054785
is the Witt vectors over
\mathbb{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 29040^{\mathchar 29032}}
. We denote by
\mathbb{\mathchar 29011}_{\mathchar 29032}
the automorphism group of
\mathbb{\mathchar 29000}_{\mathchar 29032}
.

The action of
\mathbb{\mathchar 29011}_{\mathchar 29032}
on
\mathchar 29010_{\mathchar 29032}
is extremely complicated. For instance, the associated cohomology groups
encode deep information about the stable homotopy category (see
[
4
]
, for example). In fact, it is difficult even to compute the leading term of the action. The bulk of
Chai’s paper
[
2
]
is devoted to this computation. In this paper, we consider the action of
\mathbb{\mathchar 29011}_{\mathchar 29032}
only on certain elements of
\mathchar 29010_{\mathchar 29032}
.
More specifically, we look at the induced action on the quotient of
\mathchar 29010_{\mathchar 29032}
by the invariant ideal
\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 314\mathchar 314\mathchar 314\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785
. Several efforts in this direction have been made at height
\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
. In this case, of course,
\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040\delimiter 84054785
.

In the special case
\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
and
\mathchar 29040\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28723
, an approximation of the action appears in
the 2008 paper
[
5
]
. The authors use the Lubin-Tate theory of deformations to encode the action in the coefficients of a certain power series. This approach results in a system of recursive equations that are then solved explicitly. In 2013, two independently-written papers extended the computations at height
\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
to arbitrary primes
\mathchar 29040
. One was the PhD thesis of Lader
[
8
]
, who also used Lubin-Tate theory. See also
[
1
]
for a review of Lader’s results in English, among other things.
The other
is a paper by Kohlhaase
[
7
]
, who made use of the rigid analytic period morphism of Gross and Hopkins
[
6
]
as applied to computations by Devinatz-Hopkins in
[
3
]
.

We follow the program outlined by Lader to extend the computations to all heights
\mathchar 29032
and all primes
\mathchar 29040
. As with the previous efforts, we obtain recursive formulas. We note that when evaluated at height
\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
, our formulas agree with previous computations, and provide the same level of accuracy. Finally, we specialize to
\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28723
and
\mathchar 29040\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28722
and solve our recursive equations explicitly. We note that while this final computation is long and tedious, it would not be difficult to generalize to other small heights
\mathchar 29032
, particularly with the help of computers.

2.
Organization and Main Results

Our first task is to approximate the universal deformation
\mathchar 28998
of the Honda formal group law, working at height
\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28722
. This is the main result of Section
3
, where we break the computation into two parts. We first compute the exponential
\exp\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785
of
\mathchar 28998
as the inverse of
\log\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84054785
, the logarithm of
\mathchar 28998
.

(Cf.

)
.

\displaystyle\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29049\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29040^{\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29034\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29037}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29048^{\mathchar 29040^{\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29049^{\mathchar 29040^{\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}}\delimiter 84054785^{\mathchar 29037\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 314

An arithmetic coincidence (cf. Remark
3.9
) splits the computation into two cases:
\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
and
\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28722
. In particular,
\mathchar 28998
takes a slightly different form in the case
\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
. See
[
8
]
Lemma 3.1, or
[
1
]
Theorem 3.1.

The universal deformation
\mathchar 28998
is, in particular, a formal group law with coefficients in the Lubin-Tate deformation ring
\mathchar 29010_{\mathchar 29032}
. It is universal in the sense that it induces a bijective correspondence between
\mathchar 8511
-isomorphism classes of deformations of
\mathbb{\mathchar 29000}_{\mathchar 29032}
to complete local rings and continuous ring maps out of
\mathchar 29010_{\mathchar 29032}
.
Roughly speaking, each automorphism of
\mathbb{\mathchar 29000}_{\mathchar 29032}
determines a “change of coordinates” that we may apply to
\mathchar 28998
to create a new deformation with coefficients in
\mathchar 29010_{\mathchar 29032}
. Each deformation is classified by an automorphism of
\mathchar 29010_{\mathchar 29032}
, and these automorphisms together determine the action of
\mathbb{\mathchar 29011}_{\mathchar 29032}
. See
[
11
]
for an overview.

Having an explicit formula for
\mathchar 28998
allows us to access the action directly.
In Section
4
we compute the action of an element
\mathchar 29031\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29011}_{\mathchar 29032}
in terms of a unit
\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 28720}
in
\mathchar 29010_{\mathchar 29032}
. Although we must assume
\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28722
to use Theorem
3.10
, our results agree with previous computations at
\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722
.

Before we state our results, we recall that
\mathbb{\mathchar 29011}_{\mathchar 29032}
is isomorphic to the subgroup of units in

\mathbb{\mathchar 29015}\delimiter 69632778\mathchar 29011\delimiter 86414091\delimiter 68408078\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29011^{\mathchar 29032}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29040\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29025\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29025^{\mathchar 28955}\mathchar 29011\delimiter 84054785

where
\mathbb{\mathchar 29015}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29015\delimiter 67273472\mathbb{\mathchar 28998}_{\mathchar 29040^{\mathchar 29032}}\delimiter 84054785
are the Witt vectors,
\mathchar 29025\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29015}
and
\mathchar 28955
is a lift of the Frobenius. Therefore
any
\mathchar 29031\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29011}
can be expressed as

\mathchar 29031\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29011^{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 314

We prove in Theorem
4.2
that for all
\mathchar 29032\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722

\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 8707}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 28720}^{\mathchar 29040^{\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mod\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 314\mathchar 314\mathchar 314\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785

for a unit
\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 28720}
such that
\mathchar 29044_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12817\mathchar 29031_{\mathchar 28720}\mod\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29040\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 314\mathchar 314\mathchar 314\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 29032\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\delimiter 84054785
. We remark that this computation was previously known to the stable homotopy theory community.

The rest of Section

is devoted to determining

in terms of the generators of

Remark 3.9
.

]
Lemma 3.1.

Note 4.5
.

Since the monomial degrees of
